Recorded checks
The cumulative verifier rebuilds all 47 modules and audits all 633 explicit declarations: 526
theorems and 107 definitions. Warnings are errors, unexpected axiom dependencies are rejected,
and 27 deliberately invalid proof controls are tested. The allowed transitive axioms remain
propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound. Exact results, compiler commands and source
hashes are in the accompanying machine-readable reports.
All 189 infrastructure and release tests passed. Independent exact-arithmetic mirrors
checked 3,040 normalized and 3,040 unnormalized bounds in 95 standard-window families,
6,596 source defects and 6,596 reciprocal defects, 75 product identities, 360 inverse-closure
cases, 720 Euler commutators and 150 telescoping identities. They are finite Python mirrors,
not code extracted from Lean and not substitutes for the universal proofs.
Failed development snapshots and diagnostics are preserved separately. Historical source
files and earlier supplements remain unchanged. Mathlib’s pinned compiled cache is reused,
not independently rebuilt; the original historical numerical suite is not rerun. The manuscript-
to-formal correspondence remains a mathematical review.
What is still unproved
The all-residue Theorem 7.5 requires the additional range p ≤j < mp. Its supporting prose
proof treats singular ratios at c = p, 2p, . . . , (m −1)p through weighted Amice estimates and
a dual Mahler pairing, then propagates across the intervening indices. Those actual-family
estimates and the full remaining propagation are not supplied by this release. A unit argument
at p ∤c cannot be used at p | c.
Release boundary: 23/24 named manuscript-proved items, with Theorem 7.5
still pending. The two conjectures remain unproved. The proved standard-window result
is an infinite family, but no numerical proportion of the remaining work is inferred from the
width of that window.

The 104 added declarations include 30 declarations in four previously compiled research

No hidden estimate. The final weighted-ratio theorem assumes primality, the modulus
divisibility and the specified residue bounds. It does not assume weighted section membership,
reciprocal closure, the ratio identity, a Mahler pairing or the desired higher-difference bound.
Pages 2–5 describe the constructed objects and proved bridges.
1. Integral cyclic coefficients
Work first in an arbitrary commutative ring R. If zp = 1, set
I = (p, z −1),
P = (p).
Expanding ((z −1) + 1)p = 1 and using divisibility of every interior binomial coefficient in
prime row p gives
(z −1)p ∈PI.

Every other degree-p product of the two generators of I also belongs to PI. The formal ideal
proof therefore establishes
Ip ⊆PI.
For q = p⌊q/p⌋+ (q mod p), strong induction and the factorial valuation identity give
Iq ⊆P vp(q!) .
In detail, write b = ⌊q/p⌋. Then
Iq ⊆P bIb ⊆P b+vp(b!) = P vp(q!).
No integral-domain hypothesis or division in a field is used here.

3. Weighted closure and the normalized denominator
Let w(d) = ⌊d/p⌋. Since w(i) + w(d −i) ≤w(d), the coefficient formula for a product proves
closure of Wp under multiplication. Additive closure is immediate. For U ∈Wp with U(0) = 1,
strong induction in the actual reciprocal coefficient recurrence proves
U−1 ∈Wp.
This inverse property is a theorem, not an additional field in a structure encoding weighted
series.

Differentiation and the Euler field
The derivative coefficient is (d + 1)ad+1. If p ∤d + 1, the adjacent weights agree. If p | d + 1,
the integer factor d + 1 compensates for the one-unit weight increase. Therefore ordinary
differentiation preserves Wp.
For the actual logarithm, its coefficient denominator is d, and the proved inequality
vp(d) ≤⌊d/p⌋
(d > 0)
implies log(1 + T) ∈Wp. Consequently
LT F = log(1 + T)(1 + T)F ′
also preserves Wp.
Removing a singular forced zero
Put c = ap < N and define the actual normalized kernel
Kc(t) = c!

n≥0
tNn
(Nn + c)!,
Kc(0) = 1.
Let ℓ(T) = log(1 + T)/T, defined by shifting logarithm coefficients, not by inverting T. Set
U(T) = ℓ(T)cKc(log(1 + T)).
The formal proof establishes U(0) = 1 and the exact factorization
HN,c(log(1 + T)) = T c
c! U(T).
It follows coefficientwise that
[T d]U = c![T d+c]HN,c(log(1 + T)).
Because pa | (ap)! and ⌊(d + ap)/p⌋= a + ⌊d/p⌋, the already proved section estimate gives
U ∈Wp. Thus U−1 ∈Wp as well. The factorial divisibility is derived from the pinned Legendre
valuation theorem.
Boundary. The original positive-address section has zero constant coefficient. It is not
the series to which unit inverse closure is applied.
4. The singular ratio estimate
For a positive address c, the rational ratio is independently constructed from the original
kernels:
Rc(t) = c Kc−1(t)Kc(t)−1.
The scalar factor c is necessary. The source proves the cleared identity
Rc(t)HN,c(t) = tHN,c−1(t).
This connects the constructed object to the original unnormalized ratio Ac−1/Ac. It does not
assign the desired transformed formula as its definition.

Final strengthened edition
After actual logarithmic substitution and the chain rule,
Rc(log(1 + T))HN,c(log(1 + T)) = LT HN,c(log(1 + T)).
Insert the proved factorization HN,c(log(1 + T)) = T cU/c!.
Leibniz differentiation and
cancellation of that nonzero section give
Rc(log(1 + T)) = c(1 + T)ℓ(T) + (LT U)U−1.
All differentiation, substitution, nonzeroness and cancellation obligations are proved in the
source.
For c = ap, the second summand belongs to Wp by the closure results on page 4. The
first does too: shifting the logarithm coefficient loses at most one unit of the floor weight, so
pℓ(T) ∈Wp, and the integer a supplies the remaining factor. Therefore
Rap(log(1 + T)) ∈Wp.
The final rational coefficient theorem states the bound with an explicit zero alternative. No
finite-valued default valuation at zero is used as the meaning of unlimited precision.
Checks that prevent a normalization mistake
The source proves that the actual ratio has constant coefficient c, not one. In the concrete case
N = 6, c = 3, the universal theorem gives weighted membership and the constant coefficient is
proved to be 3.
It also derives [T 3]H3,0(log(1 +T)) = 1/6 from the actual differential recurrence and checks
its valuation v3(1/6) = −1. Thus weighted membership does not mean ordinary coefficient
integrality. This value attains the allowed floor weight at degree three.
Principal target. SingularRatioAmice.paper_singular_amice_estimate. The
theorem’s assumptions contain only the declared arithmetic parameter conditions.
5. Verification and the remaining theorem
The cumulative inventory consists of 57 modules, 630 explicit theorem declarations and 128
explicit definitions. The release verifier compiles every module in dependency order into a new
directory, treats warnings as errors, and requests a transitive axiom report for all 758 listed
declarations. The allowed axioms remain propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound.
The verifier retains the previous 27 invalid-proof controls and adds two: incorrectly
promoting a weighted coefficient to an integral one, and incorrectly normalizing the singular
ratio constant to one. Missing imports, panics and timeouts do not qualify as successful
negative controls. The preserved source checker rejects proof placeholders, added axioms and
selected kernel-bypass constructs.
The Python infrastructure suite now contains 209 tests.
Four old cumulative-count
assertions initially failed against the extended inventory. Their original files and failed test
output are preserved; the historical tests now inspect the frozen eighth-block inventory where
appropriate, while current-corpus tests enforce the new counts.

What has not been proved by this block
To obtain the singular higher-difference theorem, the weighted coefficient estimate must still be
connected to the dual Mahler polynomial filtration and its integral pairing. The argument must
then establish the required ratio flatness, propagate between singular indices, and assemble the
original all-residue Euler family. None of these obligations is replaced by the finite regression
tests.
The full manuscript register remains 23/24. Neither conjecture is resolved. The original
manuscript is unchanged. Mathlib’s pinned compiled cache is reused, not independently rebuilt.
Frozen compiler logs, source hashes, failed drafts and fresh-extraction results accompany the
release; its machine-readable audit is authoritative about the runs actually performed.

4. All-residue assembly and original coefficients
The standard-window source and inverse results supply R1 and FN,0. Induction over 1 ≤c < N
now has two proved cases. At p | c, the singular weighted estimate and the new substitution
theorem give flatness. At p ∤c, the preceding Riccati propagation applies. Thus every actual
ratio Rc is flat.
Let Kj(t) be the normalized section kernel with constant coefficient one. The constructed
unnormalized Euler series is
FN,j(t) = j! Kj(t)−1.
The source/inverse identities and the factorial successor identity prove
FN,j+1 = FN,jRj+1,
FN,j = FN,0
jY
c=1
Rc.
The product formula is not an assembly hypothesis. Proved flatness of all factors and the
original product-closure theorem establish flatness of the full family.
Coefficient extraction identifies, literally,
(Nn)![tNn]FN,j = j! CN,j(n).
The support outside multiples of N is proved separately. The constructed integral lift therefore
represents the original rational coefficient function. The existing saturation criterion and the
proved step-multiple argument give the ideal bound at every order, row and multiplier. Multi-
plication by j! commutes with finite differences, giving precisely the displayed normalization
loss.

Independent finite checks
The standard-library Python mirror checks 328 complete residue families for primes 2, 3, 5, 7, 11,
including 233 higher-residue families. It checks 4,264 integral unnormalized coefficients, 22,137
normalized and 22,137 unnormalized difference bounds, and 1,640 comparisons with a separately
computed ordinary reciprocal. Further checks cover 108 weighted differential iterations, 205
integer Frobenius-square cases and 180 exact Picard agreement prefixes.
These are finite exact-arithmetic checks, not extracted Lean code and not substitutes
for the universal formal proofs. The old normalization counterexample and the new sharp
exponent-two example remain visible.

Preservation and limitations
All 57 earlier proof modules and the original 28-page status-corrected manuscript are retained
unchanged. The new supplement and amendments give the current proof route. Historical
failed drafts and interrupted recovery attempts are retained separately from accepted builds.
Historical tests that assert an older unfinished status now read that frozen historical status;
current tests require the actual unconditional capstone theorem before accepting completion.
The portable Lean and Mathlib versions are pinned. Mathlib’s compiled dependency cache
is reused, not independently rebuilt. The historical numerical experiment suite and Papers
I–VIII are not rerun. The two native conjectures remain unproved. No independent historical-
priority assessment is made. Manuscript-to-Lean correspondence remains mathematical review,
not a theorem equating natural-language prose with formal types.

E.11
Nishibuchi conjecture completion
Checkpoint scope. This complete source body retains the status of its dated original
supplement. Earlier pending or partial-coverage labels are historical; the final core records
their resolution.
1. The complete external statement
For N > 0 and 0 ≤j < N, use the original unnormalized convention

Theorem E.1. Let p be prime, m > 0, and assume m | p −1 or m = 2. Put N = mp and
q = lcm(2, p −1). Every E(N,j)
Nn
is p-adically integral. For every r ≥1,
E(N,j)
Nn+qpr ≡E(N,j)
Nn
(mod pr)
(0 ≤j < N, n ≥r).
In particular, the positive sufficient threshold n0 = r works uniformly in all allowed parameters.
This is the parameter range and displacement of Nishibuchi’s Conjecture 3.9 in
arXiv:2605.10677v2. The explicit threshold also establishes the suggested bound n0 ≤r. The
original paper already proves the p = 2, m = 2, j = 0 period as a special case, with all starting
rows; this extension does not claim that special case as a new discovery.
Principal declaration
KomorebiPaperIX.NishibuchiCompletion.conjecture_3_9
The theorem type includes only the stated arithmetic hypotheses. There is no assumed
congruence, weighted estimate, or flatness certificate. Precision is the zero-safe predicate x = 0
or vp(x) ≥r. Its connection to genuine ideal divisibility in Zp is used in the proof.
Claim boundary. This establishes a sufficient threshold and a period, not the earliest
starting index, the least period, or a pointwise optimal valuation formula. The correspondence
with the externally written statement is a mathematical review of definitions and quantifiers,
not an automated semantic-equivalence theorem. The two native conjectures in Paper IX
remain unchanged and unproved.
2. The missing dyadic branch
For odd primes, the extra option m = 2 already divides p −1. Therefore the only allowed
family outside the preceding all-residue theorem is p = 2, m = 2, with N = 4.
The new proof establishes a higher-difference estimate for every one of its four addresses:
v2

∆k
uC4,j(n)

≥min(k, n)
(0 ≤j < 4).
All natural u, k, n are allowed, including zero step, zero order and zero differences. The same
bound holds for j!C4,j(n). Both a genuine Z2 divisibility theorem and a valuation theorem for
the original rational sequence are included.

For a packed coefficient sequence e, the exact extraction formula is

Here U is the coefficient shift. The nonnegative extraction index is why the first estimate
covers k ≤n. The existing saturation lemma extends it to all k with exponent min(k, n), and
the step-multiple lemma extends step one to every natural u.
Identity with the original rational coefficients
The integral lift is constructed from the decoded coefficients of the actual reciprocal. The code
proves
coe(ej(n)) = C4,j(n)
in Q2,
and proves that the coefficient map commutes with every difference stencil. Multiplying by j!
yields the unnormalized family. The valuation result is not divisibility in a field.
Source modules: CubicDworkAlgebra, CubicDworkClosure;
DyadicFourDwork and DyadicFourCongruences.

Divisor branch
For m | p −1, the preceding all-residue theorem provides the needed differences as soon as
r ≤mn. With q = lcm(2, p −1), the required packet step q/m is proved to be an integer
multiple of (p −1)/m. The degree conversion is checked exactly:
mp

n + pr−1 q
m

= mpn + qpr.
This retains the stronger sufficient condition r ≤mn on the divisor branch. The uniform
choice n ≥r implies it for every positive m.

Exceptional branch
For p = 2, m = 2, q = 2 and the packet displacement is 2r−1. The new dyadic bound supplies
the hypothesis when n ≥r, and
4
 n + 2r−1 = 4n + 2r+1.
The p = 2, m = 1 branch is also checked using q = 2, not an incorrectly substituted q = p−1 = 1.
An exact arithmetic check, not an optimality claim
The independent rational recurrence gives C4,1(1) = −1/5 and C4,1(2) = 121/45. Their
difference is 26/9, of 2-adic valuation one. This agrees with the new bound at n = k = u = 1.
The source also contains universal instantiations of the degree-indexed theorem at all four
dyadic addresses and at an odd singular address.
Source modules: PeriodFromDifferences, NishibuchiCompletion, ConjectureExamples.
5. Verification, provenance and boundary
The release has 79 active modules: 70 unchanged baseline modules and nine new modules.
Its inventory contains 836 named theorem declarations, 162 explicit definitions and two
abbreviations. The transitive audit includes all 1,000 of these declarations. The increase of 83
named theorems includes supporting lemmas and examples, not 83 independent discoveries.
A clean cumulative run compiled all 79 modules, matched all 1,000 axiom reports,
verified all nine dependency revisions, and correctly rejected all 37 invalid-proof controls.
The statement-contract file separately checked the external parameter disjunction, integrality,
positive threshold, and exact degree displacement. Warnings were treated as errors.
The capstone depends only on propext, Classical.choice, and Quot.sound. The 25
extension-infrastructure tests passed. Exact-rational checks passed 6,500 higher-difference
cases in each dyadic normalization, 588 dyadic periods and 1,690 divisor-branch periods. These
finite checks are not the universal proof. A fresh-extraction replay, when recorded in the
separate release audit, is additional evidence beyond the source build.
Reproduction
The portable runtime is the pinned Linux x86-64 Lean 4.34.0-rc2 kit. It is not duplicated
inside this source archive. From the extracted release, run
bash Run-Portable.sh /path/to/Komorebi_Portable_Lean_4.34.0-rc2
The launcher checks file hashes, runs 25 extension-infrastructure tests and the new exact-
arithmetic regressions, then builds every module in a new output directory and audits every
registered declaration. It also compiles an explicit statement contract and negative controls.
The final check verifies the released snapshot again.
The source-policy scan excludes proof placeholders and additional project axioms. Compiler
errors, timeouts, missing imports and invalid negative-control preludes are not accepted as
successful theorem verification. Failed development snapshots are retained separately from
active proof sources.
Komorebi P.

Euler congruences / Paper IX

Limits of the completion claim
The pinned Mathlib compiled cache is reused, not rebuilt from source. No independent
third-party checker or peer review was run. The original numerical experiment suite and
Papers I–VIII were not rerun. Prior verification evidence is retained as historical evidence and
is not relabeled as fresh testing. The original Paper IX manuscript still requires cumulative
supplements and amendments; this extension is not a rewritten primary manuscript.
The external Conjecture 3.9 is distinct from Paper IX’s native Conjectures 13.2 and 13.3.
Those two remain unproved. The present result does not assert least periods, earliest thresholds,
or an optimal exponent for every individual parameter tuple. It makes no historical-priority
claim relative to other unpublished work.
Reference
Yuta Nishibuchi. p-adic Congruences of Generalized Euler Numbers and Relations to Even
Zeta Values. arXiv:2605.10677v2, revised 1 June 2026. Definition 2.1, Conjecture 3.9 and
Proposition 3.10. The version identifier is fixed for the statement comparison.
